Class decisions (numbered; rows cite them as "log §4 class decision N"):

Cite one in a row's notes as "log §4 class decision N". Every statute word below is quoted from the corpus with its citation; open the section yourself (sec.py) before you quote it in a row.

**CD1. The default rule on waivers.** Illinois has no URLTA and no general statute that voids waivers in a residential lease. A waiver of a rule Illinois does not have is empty, not lawful, so a waiver topic states the default rule first. The specific bars found so far, each to be opened before it is relied on:
- 765 ILCS 705/1(a): a lease term "exempting the lessor from liability for damages for injuries to person or property caused by or resulting from the negligence of the lessor" is "void as against public policy and wholly unenforceable".
- 735 ILCS 5/9-108: in residential cases "either party may demand trial by jury, notwithstanding any waiver of jury trial contained in any lease or contract".
- 765 ILCS 750/30: the Safe Homes Act "may not be waived or modified in any lease or separate agreement".
- 735 ILCS 5/9-218(b): a lease provision conflicting with the on-site payment rule is "void and unenforceable".
- 765 ILCS 705/35(b)-(c): fee transparency and the junk-fee ban (CD3).
- 765 ILCS 705/4(b)-(c): no required electronic funds transfer; a violation is "an unlawful practice under the Consumer Fraud and Deceptive Business Practices Act".

The Landlord Retaliation Act (765 ILCS 721), the Security Deposit Return Act (765 ILCS 710), the Security Deposit Interest Act (765 ILCS 715) and the repair-and-deduct act (765 ILCS 742) contain no anti-waiver sentence. Screen this before relying on it; the main pass's screen was `\bwaiv\w*|\bvoid\b|unenforceable` over those acts. Whether a lease waiver of their rights is unenforceable as against public policy is case law, not read. The cautious course: never draft a clause that waives or narrows a tenant's statutory right.

**CD2. Penalty versus liquidated damages** is case law, not read in this pass (IL log §7). The cautious course:
- Charge the actual, documented cost.
- Use a preset amount only where a statute authorizes it. 765 ILCS 710/1(a) is the main one: "If a written lease specifies the cost for cleaning, repair, or replacement of any component of the leased premises ... the lessor may withhold the dollar amount specified in the lease" (see `deposit-cost-schedule-il`).
- Otherwise, a preset amount is used only as a reasonable pre-estimate and only if the CD3 first-page rule is met.

**CD3. The rental fee law, 765 ILCS 705/35 (P.A. 104-479).**
- First page: "All non-optional fees ... shall be explicitly contained on the first page of a lease agreement. If a fee is not explicitly contained on the first page of a lease agreement, a tenant shall not be liable for payment of such fee" (705/35(b)).
- Junk-fee ban: the barred fees are listed in (c)(1)-(11).
- Renaming: "A landlord may not rename a fee or charge to avoid application of this Section" (705/35(e)).
- Scope: the section applies to leases "entered into after the effective date", and it excludes owner-occupied premises with 6 units or fewer (705/35(e)).
- Remedy: a civil action with "injunctive relief, monetary relief, attorney's fees, and costs" (705/35(f)).
- Effective date (main pass, settled as far as the text allows): the enrolled Act's Section 99 says 'This Act takes effect July 1, 2026'. The bill passed both houses on 2026-04-08 and the Governor approved it on 2026-06-26. Under 5 ILCS 75/1(b), a bill passed before June 1 that names an effective date 'shall become effective on that date if that date is the same as or subsequent to the date the bill becomes a law', which points to July 1, 2026. The General Assembly's records and the ILCS source note ('eff. 1-1-27') say January 1, 2027. No later Act settles it. Helpers write: "P.A. 104-479 (effective July 1, 2026 under its Section 99, or January 1, 2027 as the General Assembly's records state; the cautious course treats it as applying to leases entered into on or after July 1, 2026)". Cite 5 ILCS 75/1 only after opening it.
- The library applies the fee box now (Taylor, 2026-09-30). The builder puts every non-optional charge in the first-page fee box (`fee-disclosure-first-page-il`).
- For each fee topic, say whether the fee is barred, allowed only if on the first page, or untouched.

**CD4. Local law.** Name the ordinances you meet, but don't research them (rule 3): the Chicago Residential Landlord and Tenant Ordinance, the Cook County Residential Tenant Landlord Ordinance, Evanston, Urbana, Mount Prospect. Home-rule units keep wide powers (Ill. Const. art. VII, § 6). Preemption sections such as 765 ILCS 742/30 and 765 ILCS 705/35(d) are state law and are read.

**CD5. Case law** is not read in this pass. A row whose answer turns on case law says "case law not read" and gives the cautious course. This covers:
- the implied warranty of habitability and its waiver;
- penalty doctrine;
- constructive eviction;
- unconscionability;
- whether the Consumer Fraud Act reaches a residential lease (CD7).

**CD6. Money claims.**
- A claim for rent may be joined in an eviction: "a claim for rent may be joined in the complaint, and judgment may be entered for the amount of rent found due" (735 ILCS 5/9-106(f)). Otherwise "no matters not germane to the distinctive purpose of the proceeding shall be introduced by joinder, counterclaim or otherwise" (same section).
- Damages beyond rent go in a separate civil action or small claims. The main pass read the court rules in the browser on 2026-11-08 (full Supreme Court Rules PDF, "Current October 21, 2026") and saved Rules 281, 282, 285, 288, 139 and 99.2 to the sources folder. Cite them as court rules:
  - Rule 281: 'a small claim is a civil action based on either tort or contract for money not in excess of $10,000, exclusive of interest and costs' (Ill. S. Ct. R. 281).
  - Rule 282(b): 'No corporation may appear as claimant, assignee, subrogee or counterclaimant in a small claims proceeding, unless represented by counsel.' (Ill. S. Ct. R. 282)
  - Rule 139(b) requires an eviction complaint to attach the notice or demand, with proof of service, and for a 735 ILCS 5/9-210 case on a written lease, the lease or its relevant parts (Ill. S. Ct. R. 139).
  - Rule 99.2: circuits may run residential eviction mediation programs (Ill. S. Ct. R. 99.2, eff. March 1, 2026).
  - Any other rule a row needs: file a `fetch` object naming the rule number.

**CD7. The Consumer Fraud Act (815 ILCS 505).**
- Merchandise: "The term "merchandise" includes any objects, wares, goods, commodities, intangibles, real estate situated outside the State of Illinois, or services" (815 ILCS 505/1(b)).
- Trade and commerce: "The terms "trade" and "commerce" mean the advertising, offering for sale, sale, or distribution of any services and any property, tangible or intangible, real, personal or mixed" (815 ILCS 505/1(f)).
- Whether the Act's general prohibition (815 ILCS 505/2) reaches an Illinois residential lease is case law, not read.
- Express hooks into the Act: 765 ILCS 705/4(c) (electronic funds transfer), and any other you find; quote it.
- The cautious course is to treat deceptive leasing conduct as exposed under the Act.

**CD8. Arbitration** is the main pass's decision. `arbitration-agreement` is not offered in Illinois. 765 ILCS 705/35(f) gives "Any person alleging a violation of this Section" a civil action for monetary relief and attorney's fees, and a violation includes a lease agreement that requires a barred fee ("No landlord or lease agreement may require ...", 705/35(c)). That penalty attaches to including a prohibited term (Taylor's rule, the Delaware/Virginia reading). The main pass writes `edu-binding-arbitration-il`.

**CD9. Notices.** Article IX service is by the methods in 735 ILCS 5/9-211. The 5-day rent demand is 735 ILCS 5/9-209, the 10-day notice for other defaults is 735 ILCS 5/9-210, and periodic notices are in 735 ILCS 5/9-205 and 5/9-207. Existing rows already answer these topics (`edu-notices-to-quit-il`, `edu-nonpayment-notice-il`); point to them.

**CD10. Absence wording.**
- A Confirmed-absent body names its corpus: "the Illinois Compiled Statutes, the Illinois Constitution and the list of the 104th General Assembly's Public Acts", and what was not searched: the Administrative Code, court rules, local ordinances and case law (unless the row read one).
- The notes give the battery line from `bat.py cite <ID>`.
- Not located (the subject is outside the corpus): name the missing source, the section or rule that would answer the topic, what was proved inside the corpus, and what applies in its place. Also file a `fetch` object.

**CD11. Sources outside the corpus:** the Illinois Administrative Code, Supreme Court rules, IDHR publications, standardized court forms and federal law.
- Helpers do not browse. File a `fetch` object naming the exact title, part and section, or the U.S.C. or C.F.R. section, and continue.
- The main pass reads and saves it in the browser, then hands the text back.
- A row resting on an unread source is NEEDS_REVIEW until then.

**CD12. Session laws.** The ILCS text is compiled through P.A. 104-854, the last Act on the 104th General Assembly's list (fetched 2026-11-08), so the compiled text carries every enacted change through that Act. A delayed-effective section prints both versions. Quote the version in force on 2026-11-08 and give the other version's Public Act and effective date.

**CD13. Leads found by the main pass (open them before relying on them).**
- The Service Member Residential Property Act (330 ILCS 62) sets a servicemember's right to end a residential rental agreement. Its 5-25 says the tenant 'is not liable for any other rent or any liquidated damages due to the early termination' (330 ILCS 62/5-25). It runs alongside 765 ILCS 705/16. It matters for statutory-early-termination and the servicemember topics.
- The Bedbug Inspection Act (815 ILCS 309) binds merchants who rent furniture or electronic equipment, not landlords. Rows on furnishings or bed bugs may say so.
- Condominium and common-interest-community leasing: 765 ILCS 605/18(n), 765 ILCS 605/9.2, 765 ILCS 160/1-35(a) and 735 ILCS 5/9-104.2 (a board may evict a tenant when the unit owner defaults). The main pass answers association-obligations-disclosure. For other topics, point to `edu-association-obligations-disclosure-il`.
- P.A. 104-479's effective date: see CD3.
- Utility rules read by the main pass (2026-11-09, ilga.gov JCAR database, which says it is not the official text): 83 Ill. Adm. Code 280.40 (deposits: residential cap of ⅙ of estimated annual charges, interest, automatic refund after 12 months of good payment, refund within 30 days) and 83 Ill. Adm. Code 280.35 (landlord/property manager agreements for service between tenants must be in writing; 'The utility shall not hold the landlord/property manager responsible for an amount owing to the utility by any tenant.'). Saved in sources/; quote them for utility-transfer and utility topics (slice H). Federal HUD rules 24 C.F.R. §§ 982.308, 982.310, 982.451, 966.4(b), 966.6 are saved too.

### §6 Findings on existing rows

1. `edu-habitability-il`: The row says "Illinois has no general statute that sets a residential landlord's repair or habitability duty" and lists the statutes that add specific duties, but not 310 ILCS 65/10.5 (P.A. 103-248, eff. 6-30-23), which makes habitability standards a funding condition: 'All housing financed under the Illinois Affordable Housing Program must meet a minimum standard of living requirement', including 'Premises of the single-family or multi-family housing must be kept fit for human habitation', heat of at least 68 degrees by day and 62 at night in the heating season, no heat lapse over 24 hours, and pest control (310 ILCS 65/10.5). The row's "no general statute" sentence stays true. Suggested addition to the list of specific duties: "minimum living standards (including heat, cooling, pest control and keeping the premises fit for human habitation) for housing financed under the Illinois Affordable Housing Program". Found by Battery IL-A-002 ("fit for habitation"); its whole-code battery 41 of 2026-09-30 did not list 310 ILCS 65/10.5.
2. `edu-servicemember-rights-il`: The row cites 765 ILCS 705/16 and 330 ILCS 63 but not the Service Member Residential Property Act, 330 ILCS 62 (P.A. 96-1400, eff. 7-29-10, read whole 2026-11-08), a separate lease-termination statute with different terms: it covers a lessee who enters "military service" (defined in 330 ILCS 62/5-5 as federal service or active duty 'for a period of not less than 180 days' or State active duty on orders) or receives PCS or 90-day deployment orders (330 ILCS 62/5-10); the immediate family or dependents may terminate if the lessee 'is killed in action or while on active duty' (330 ILCS 62/5-15); termination is by written notice with a copy of the orders by hand, private carrier or return-receipt mail, effective, for monthly rent, '30 days after the first date on which the next rental payment is due and payable after the date on which the notice under subsection (b) of this Section is delivered' (330 ILCS 62/5-20(c)); prepaid rent is refunded within 30 days and 'The tenant is not liable for any other rent or any liquidated damages due to the early termination' (330 ILCS 62/5-25); and 'A person who is aggrieved by a violation of this Act shall have a right of action in circuit court to enforce the provisions of this Act and in doing so may recover attorney's fees and costs' (330 ILCS 62/5-30). Suggested addition to the body: "A separate Illinois law, the Service Member Residential Property Act, gives a similar right to a servicemember entering military service of 180 days or more (or receiving qualifying orders), and to the family of one killed on active duty; on termination under it the tenant owes prorated rent and damage the tenant caused, but no other rent and no liquidated damages, and a tenant may sue to enforce it and recover attorney's fees." Also add 330 ILCS 62/5-30 to "edu-attorney-fees-il"'s list of tenant fee-award statutes.
3. `early-termination-ks`: IL segment: the tag note answers the fee law (the fee is the tenant's option, not a barred renewal or modification fee) but not log §4 class decision 2 (penalty doctrine, case law not read; preset amounts only as a reasonable pre-estimate) or class decision 3's first-page rule. The clause fixes the fee at "one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater", a figure carried from the Kansas row, not the landlord's estimate; on a long remaining term the 30% limb can far exceed one month's rent. No Illinois statute authorizes the amount (Battery IL-A-011), and 'If a fee is not explicitly contained on the first page of a lease agreement, a tenant shall not be liable for payment of such fee' (765 ILCS 705/35(b)) applies if the fee counts as non-optional. Recommendation for the main pass: record in the IL segment that the amount must be a reasonable pre-estimate under class decision 2 and that the builder lists the fee in the first-page fee box ("fee-disclosure-first-page-il"); if that is not enough, decline the tag for IL and write an IL row with a bracketed amount ("[state a reasonable estimate of your re-renting costs]"). Also note 330 ILCS 62/5-25(b) (no liquidated damages on a servicemember termination), which the clause's savings sentence reaches only through "any other termination right or limitation provided by applicable law".
4. `edu-no-security-deposit-cap-il`: Its notes say the row 'Answers security-deposit-holding ... for IL: no Illinois statute' (library text). That is overtaken: the Real Estate License Act makes '"Escrow moneys" includes, without limitation, earnest moneys and security deposits, except those security deposits in which the person holding the security deposit is also the sole owner of the property being leased' (225 ILCS 454/1-10) and requires licensees to keep them in a special account separate from personal and business accounts (225 ILCS 454/20-20(a)(17)). Slice B answers security-deposit-holding with its own row, "edu-security-deposit-holding-il" (Present), and deposit-installments, deposit-last-month-rent, fee-in-lieu-of-deposit and holding-deposit with their own rows (rule 27). Suggested wording for the IL notes sentence: "Deposit holding, installments, last month's rent, fee-in-lieu and holding deposits are answered by their own IL rows (edu-security-deposit-holding-il, edu-no-deposit-installments-il, edu-no-deposit-last-month-rent-il, edu-no-fee-in-lieu-of-deposit-il, edu-no-holding-deposit-rule-il)." No change to the body is needed.
5. `edu-no-flag-display-rule-il`: The row's notes say the Condominium Property Act and Common Interest Community Association Act display provisions (765 ILCS 605, 765 ILCS 160) were 'not read', and the body says "Condominium and common-interest association rules on displays for unit owners are not covered here." Read 2026-11-08: 765 ILCS 605/18.4(h) ('No rule or regulation shall prohibit any reasonable accommodation for religious practices, including the attachment of religiously mandated objects to the front-door area of a condominium unit') and 765 ILCS 605/18.6(a) and 765 ILCS 160/1-70(a) (a board 'may not prohibit the display of the American flag or a military flag' of a unit owner). The row's statement about tenants against landlords stays correct. Suggested change to the notes only: replace "not read" with "read 2026-11-08: association rules protect unit owners' American and military flags (765 ILCS 605/18.6, 765 ILCS 160/1-70) and religious door-area objects in condominiums (765 ILCS 605/18.4(h)); see edu-religious-cultural-display-il".
6. `edu-sex-offender-landlord-il`: Statute: 'It is unlawful for a child sex offender who owns and resides at residential real estate to knowingly rent any residential unit within the same building in which he or she resides to a person who is the parent or guardian of a child or children under 18 years of age. This subsection shall apply only to leases or other rental arrangements entered into after January 1, 2009' (720 ILCS 5/11-9.3(c-6)); 'A person who violates this Section is guilty of a Class 4 felony' (720 ILCS 5/11-9.3(f)). The row says: "Such a landlord may also refuse to rent a unit in the building where they live to a parent or guardian of a minor child without violating the Human Rights Act." That is true (775 ILCS 5/3-106(J)) but leaves out that renting to such a person is itself a felony, so a landlord reading it would think refusal is optional. Suggested wording: "Such a landlord may not knowingly rent another unit in the building where they live to a parent or guardian of a child under 18 (a Class 4 felony, for leases entered into after January 1, 2009), and refusing to rent to such a person does not violate the Human Rights Act." Add 720 ILCS 5/11-9.3(c-6), (f) to the notes.
7. `edu-assistance-animals-il`: The row's notes say it answers service-animal-denial-penalty as "a civil rights violation, no criminal penalty for housing located (battery 86)". Two criminal access statutes exist and may reach parts of rental property: the White Cane Law, under which one who 'denies or interferes with admittance to or enjoyment of the public facilities enumerated in Section 3' is 'guilty of a Class A misdemeanor' (775 ILCS 30/4), the facilities including 'hotels, lodging places, places of public accommodation, amusement or resort and other places to which the general public is invited' (775 ILCS 30/3); and 720 ILCS 5/48-8 (Class C misdemeanor for denying a service animal entry to a public place of accommodation as defined in 775 ILCS 5/5-101, which includes 'other place of lodging' and 'other sales or rental establishment'). Their reach to dwellings is case law, not read. Suggested notes change: replace the parenthetical with "(a civil rights violation in housing; criminal access statutes for public places, 775 ILCS 30/3-4 and 720 ILCS 5/48-8, may reach leasing offices and areas open to the public; see edu-service-animal-denial-penalty-il)". The body is not wrong and needs no change.
8. `pet-policy-il`: Statute: a tenant of a multifamily rental unit of 500 square feet or more 'acquired, constructed, or rehabilitated with any money from the Trust Fund after January 1, 2022 and that was designated for affordable housing for low and very low-income families shall be allowed to keep at least 2 cats or one dog that weighs under 50 pounds regardless of breed or height within the tenant's residence' (310 ILCS 65/10(o)); not for service animals, dangerous or vicious dogs, supportive living facilities or elderly housing (same subsection); policy terms in 310 ILCS 65/18(a). The clause's prompt "[list approved pets, or state that no pets are permitted]" lets the builder write a no-pets lease, which is unlawful for those units, and the notes do not mention the rule. Suggested wording for the bracket: "[list approved pets, or state that no pets are permitted; if the unit was acquired, built or rehabilitated with Illinois Affordable Housing Trust Fund money after January 1, 2022 and is designated for low- and very low-income families (500 square feet or more, not elderly housing or supportive living), you must allow at least 2 cats or one dog under 50 pounds]" (rule 26: the condition goes in the text, not the notes). See the proposed row edu-trust-fund-pets-il.
9. `edu-no-just-cause-il`: The row says "Illinois has no statewide law requiring a landlord to have good cause to end or not renew a residential tenancy". That stays true for private housing generally, but the Illinois Affordable Housing Act sets a good-cause rule for one class of housing: 'Trust Fund assisted multi-family housing is prohibited from evicting tenants without good cause' (310 ILCS 65/10, subsection (i)); the row does not mention it. Suggested addition after the first sentence: "One exception: multi-family housing assisted by the Illinois Affordable Housing Trust Fund may not evict tenants without good cause." Found by Battery IL-D-006.
10. `edu-lead-il`: The row is accurate as far as it goes, but its notes say "410 ILCS 45/9 (mitigation notices) not read" and it leaves out two owner duties and a tenant protection in the same Act. 'The owner of a regulated facility who has received a mitigation notice under Section 9 of this Act shall post notices at all entrances to the regulated facility specifying the identified lead hazards' (410 ILCS 45/9.4); the State's Attorney 'shall take additional measures to ensure that rent is withheld from the owner by the occupants of the dwelling units affected, until the mitigation requirements under Section 9 of this Act are complied with' (410 ILCS 45/10); and 'No tenant shall be evicted because rent is withheld under the provisions of this Act' (410 ILCS 45/10). Suggested addition at the end of the body: "An owner who receives a mitigation notice must also post the Department's notices at every building entrance until the hazard is mitigated; if the owner fails to mitigate, the State's Attorney may have tenants of the affected units withhold rent until the owner complies, and no tenant may be evicted for withholding rent under the Act." Mitigation deadlines (30 days with a child 6 or younger or a pregnant person in the unit, otherwise 90) are in 410 ILCS 45/9. Full treatment in edu-lead-state-notices-il.
11. `edu-habitability-il`: Statute: 'A person commits criminal housing management when, having personal management or control of residential real estate, whether as a legal or equitable owner or as a managing agent or otherwise, he or she recklessly permits the physical condition or facilities of the residential real estate to become or remain in any condition which endangers the health or safety of a person other than the defendant' (720 ILCS 5/12-5.1(a)), a Class A misdemeanor, then a Class 4 felony (720 ILCS 5/12-5.1(b)); aggravated where the condition contributes to a death and is recklessly concealed (720 ILCS 5/12-5.1a). and the State's Attorney injunction and rent escrow in 720 ILCS 5/12-5.2(a), (b), (f) (quoted in "edu-no-repair-escrow-exemption-il"). The row says "Illinois has no general statute that sets a residential landlord's repair or habitability duty" and lists only alarms, 55-and-older heating, utilities, Cook County rekeying and repair and deduct. It omits the one statewide statute that penalizes a landlord's failure to keep a rental safe; the habitability batteries (IL 41, 42, IL-A-002) searched habitab words and could not match it. Suggested added sentence: "It is also a crime (criminal housing management) for an owner or manager to recklessly let a residential property's physical condition or facilities become or remain in a condition that endangers anyone's health or safety, and the State's Attorney may obtain an order diverting rents into a court escrow for repairs." Add 720 ILCS 5/12-5.1, 720 ILCS 5/12-5.1a and 720 ILCS 5/12-5.2 to the notes. Slice A's new rows "edu-habitability-waiver-il", "edu-habitability-modifiable-il" and "edu-landlord-breach-remedy-il" share the gap; the main pass may wish to add the same sentence there.
12. `edu-lead-il`: Statute: Lead: the State's Attorney 'shall take additional measures to ensure that rent is withheld from the owner by the occupants of the dwelling units affected, until the mitigation requirements under Section 9 of this Act are complied with' and 'No tenant shall be evicted because rent is withheld under the provisions of this Act' (410 ILCS 45/10). The State's Attorney 'has the authority to charge the owner with a Class A misdemeanor' (410 ILCS 45/10). The row describes the brochure, mitigation-notice and certificate duties (410 ILCS 45/9.1) but not the rent-withholding consequence or the eviction bar, which a landlord needs. Suggested added sentence: "If you violate the Act, the State's Attorney may charge you with a Class A misdemeanor and must take measures so that tenants of the affected units withhold rent from you until the mitigation is done; no tenant may be evicted because rent is withheld or because of action the Act requires of you." Add 410 ILCS 45/10 to the notes. Slice A's "edu-landlord-breach-remedy-il" lists tenant remedies and omits this one too.
13. `edu-unit-count-thresholds-il`: The row lists Illinois rules that turn on the size or type of the property but omits the Swimming Facility Act's threshold: a swimming pool 'includes: pools for community use, pools at apartments, condominiums, and other groups or associations having 5 or more living units' (210 ILCS 125/3.01), and 'it shall be unlawful for any person to open, establish, maintain or operate a swimming facility within this State without first obtaining a license' (210 ILCS 125/4). The row's words: "Several Illinois rules turn on the size or type of the property ... Each rule is described in its own row." Suggested addition to the list: "a pool, spa or bathing beach at an apartment or condominium building or association with 5 or more living units must be licensed under the Swimming Facility Act", with 210 ILCS 125/3.01 and 210 ILCS 125/4 added to the notes and a pointer to "edu-pool-licensing-il". The 2026-09-30 IL log's pool-safety entry (Confirmed absent, battery 34) should be corrected the same way.
14. `edu-no-meth-disclosure-il`: The row says "No Illinois statute requires a residential landlord to disclose prior methamphetamine manufacture or contamination to a tenant" and its notes say "The Residential Real Property Disclosure Act (sales) not read". That Act 'applies to any transfer by sale, exchange, installment land sale contract, assignment of beneficial interest, lease with an option to purchase, ground lease, or assignment of ground lease of residential real property' (765 ILCS 77/10), for property of 1 to 4 units, cooperative units and condominium units (765 ILCS 77/5), and its form's item 24 reads 'I am aware that this property has been used for the manufacture of methamphetamine as defined in Section 10 of the Methamphetamine Control and Community Protection Act' (765 ILCS 77/35); the report must be delivered 'before the signing of a contract' (765 ILCS 77/20). Suggested wording to add: "If your lease gives the tenant an option to buy a property of 1 to 4 units, or a condominium or cooperative unit, the Residential Real Property Disclosure Act's seller report applies, and it asks whether you are aware the property was used to manufacture methamphetamine." The same lease-with-option point bears on the mold and stigmatized-property rows only where the form asks about the condition (it does not ask about mold or deaths).
15. `edu-servicemember-rights-il`: The row rests only on 765 ILCS 705/16 and 735 ILCS 5/9-107.10 and omits the Service Member Residential Property Act (330 ILCS 62, read whole by slice G; class decision 13). That Act gives the lessee a separate termination right (330 ILCS 62/5-15: the lessee 'may, at the lessee's option, terminate the lease at any time after (i) the lessee's entry into military service or (ii) the date of the lessee's military orders'), lets the immediate family or dependents terminate if the lessee 'is killed in action or while on active duty' (330 ILCS 62/5-15), sets delivery methods 'by hand delivery, (ii) by private business carrier, or (iii) by placing the written notice in the United States mail in an envelope with sufficient postage and with return receipt requested' (330 ILCS 62/5-20(b)), requires refund of advance rent 'within 30 days after the effective date of the termination of the lease' (330 ILCS 62/5-25(a)) and provides 'The tenant is not liable for any other rent or any liquidated damages due to the early termination' (330 ILCS 62/5-25(b)), with a right of action and attorney's fees (330 ILCS 62/5-30). Its 'military service' definition uses a 180-day training period (330 ILCS 62/5-5), unlike the 29-day test in 765 ILCS 705/16. The row's words 'may terminate a residential lease, as may a family member living with the servicemember, by giving the landlord copies of the orders' are right for 765 ILCS 705/16 but incomplete. Suggested added sentence: "A second Illinois statute, the Service Member Residential Property Act, also lets a service member who enters military service, or receives orders for a permanent change of station or a deployment of at least 90 days, end a residential lease by written notice with a copy of the orders (and lets the immediate family end it if the service member is killed on active duty); the tenant then owes rent only to the termination date and no other rent or liquidated damages, though still liable for damage the tenant caused, and advance rent must be refunded within 30 days." Cross-reference: edu-statutory-early-termination-il (slice G).
16. `edu-foreclosure-tenants-il`: The row omits two statutory rules on its subject, read by slice G (735 ILCS 5/15-1508.5, 735 ILCS 5/15-1703, 735 ILCS 5/15-1704, whole sections saved). (1) A successor who fails to give occupants the required written notice 'may not collect any rent due and owing from a known occupant, or terminate a known occupant's tenancy for non-payment of such rent, until' the notice is served (735 ILCS 5/15-1508.5(d)(i), and the same for a mortgagee in possession, 735 ILCS 5/15-1703(a-5)(5)(i), and a receiver, 735 ILCS 5/15-1704(f)); and an occupant who already paid the current period's rent to the mortgagor or manager 'shall not be held liable for that rent' and may not be terminated for non-payment for that period (735 ILCS 5/15-1703(a-5)(5)(ii), with the parallels in the other two sections). (2) A foreclosure receiver 'shall accept all rental payments from an occupant of the mortgaged property, and any payments from a third party or any rental assistance program in support of an occupant's housing' (735 ILCS 5/15-1704(c)(2.5)). The row says only that a holder or purchaser who receives deposits 'must post a notice on each unit within 21 days'. Suggested added sentences: "A foreclosure buyer, mortgagee in possession or receiver who doesn't give occupants the required written notice may not collect rent from a known occupant, or end the tenancy for not paying it, until the notice is served, and an occupant who already paid the current period's rent to the owner or manager isn't liable for it again. A receiver must accept rent from occupants and payments from a third party or a rental assistance program toward an occupant's housing." Slice G's edu-rent-demand-bar-il and edu-redemption-il state these rules for their topics.
17. `edu-rent-receipts-il`: The row says "No Illinois statute requires the landlord of a house or apartment to give rent receipts (the Mobile Home Landlord and Tenant Rights Act has its own rule for mobile homes)." A whole-act screen of 765 ILCS 745 for "receipt" (corpus-IL-20261108) returns only 765 ILCS 745/3, 745/6, 745/6.7, 745/6.28, 745/6.31 and 745/22, and none of them requires a rent receipt: 765 ILCS 745/6 uses the word only for 'after receipt of the offer for a written lease' (765 ILCS 745/6(b)) and for notices; 765 ILCS 745/3 defines a park owner as one who 'directly or indirectly receives rents and has no obligation to deliver the whole of such receipts to another person' (765 ILCS 745/3). The parenthetical appears unsupported by the compiled Act. Suggested wording: "No Illinois statute requires a landlord, including a mobile home park owner, to give rent receipts." The absence otherwise holds (battery IL-J-002, "receipt for payment" and "receipt near cash payment").
18. `edu-rent-receipts-il`: The row says "No Illinois statute requires the landlord of a house or apartment to give rent receipts (the Mobile Home Landlord and Tenant Rights Act has its own rule for mobile homes)." A whole-act screen of 765 ILCS 745 for "receipt" (corpus-IL-20261108) returns only 765 ILCS 745/3, 745/6, 745/6.7, 745/6.28, 745/6.31 and 745/22, and none of them requires a rent receipt: 765 ILCS 745/6 uses the word only for 'after receipt of the offer for a written lease' (765 ILCS 745/6(b)) and for notices; 765 ILCS 745/3 defines a park owner as one who 'directly or indirectly receives rents and has no obligation to deliver the whole of such receipts to another person' (765 ILCS 745/3). The parenthetical appears unsupported by the compiled Act. Suggested wording: "No Illinois statute requires a landlord, including a mobile home park owner, to give rent receipts." The absence otherwise holds (battery IL-J-002, "receipt for payment" and "receipt near cash payment").
19. `keys-il`: Illinois item 6, first-page fees. The clause charges the tenant the cost of re-keying when keys are not returned or a replacement is needed. This is not a barred fee: it is triggered by the tenant's loss, not by 'the routine maintenance and upkeep of the unit' (765 ILCS 705/35(c)(9)). The clause's exception already keeps the between-tenancies rekey the landlord must do in Cook County (765 ILCS 705/15(b), (f)). It is a charge that applies only if an event occurs, not a 'non-optional' fee under 765 ILCS 705/35(b). But 'A landlord may not rename a fee or charge to avoid application of this Section' (765 ILCS 705/35(e)), and whether a cost-based charge is 'explicitly contained on the first page' when no amount is stated is unsettled. Builder (IL log §10.4): when keys-il is in the lease, list it in the first-page fee box among charges that apply only if a stated event occurs, as "Lost or unreturned keys: the actual cost of re-keying the affected locks, up to [amount]". A stated maximum is the cautious course. No text change to keys-il.
20. `parking-vehicle-rules`: Illinois item 6, first-page fees. The clause lets the landlord charge for "parking tags, decals, or access cards". If every tenant must pay, it is a non-optional fee, and a tenant is not liable for it unless it is 'explicitly contained on the first page of a lease agreement' (765 ILCS 705/35(b)); the IL tag note already says so. It is not on the barred list in 765 ILCS 705/35(c). Builder: where the landlord will charge for tags, decals or cards, require the amount and list it in the first-page fee box. Where no amount is entered, the cost cannot be collected under a lease the fee law covers. No text change or override needed; the condition is a builder rule, and the clause's 'may' does not oblige any charge.
21. `hoa-compliance`: Illinois item 6. Checked against 765 ILCS 605/18(n) and the Common Interest Community Association Act. 765 ILCS 160/1-35 as amended by P.A. 104-734 (eff. January 1, 2027) keeps subsection (a) word for word and adds only (d)(8), a resale disclosure of the collection policy. Both acts make the association's rules 'applicable to any person leasing a unit' and 'deemed to be incorporated in any lease' (765 ILCS 605/18(n); 765 ILCS 160/1-35(a)), so the tenant-compliance sentence restates the law and is lawful. The fines sentence passes through fines the association levies on the owner 'after notice and an opportunity to be heard' (765 ILCS 605/18.4(l); 765 ILCS 160/1-30(g)). That is a conditional charge, not a barred fee. Builder: list "association fines caused by Tenant's violation" in the first-page fee box with the conditional charges. No text change. New row "edu-association-obligations-disclosure-il" answers association-obligations-disclosure.
22. `edu-condo-leasing-il`: Illinois item 6. The row covers only 765 ILCS 605/18(n)(i)-(ii)'s first sentence. It omits three things: (1) the Common Interest Community Association Act's parallel rule ('shall be applicable to any person leasing a unit and shall be deemed to be incorporated in any lease', 765 ILCS 160/1-35(a)); (2) the association's remedies against the tenant ('an association may seek to enjoin a tenant from occupying a unit or seek to evict a tenant' for the owner's failure to follow the leasing rules, and 'The board of managers may proceed directly against a tenant', 765 ILCS 605/18(n)); (3) the board's power to evict a tenant when the owner defaults (735 ILCS 5/9-104.2(a-5)). Proposed sentences to add: "Units in a common interest community are treated the same way under the Common Interest Community Association Act. If you do not follow the leasing rules, a condominium association may sue you and your tenant together to keep the tenant out or evict the tenant, the board may sue a tenant directly for breaking the association's rules, and a condominium board may evict your tenant if you fall behind on assessments." The notes would add 765 ILCS 160/1-35(a), 765 ILCS 605/9.2 and 735 ILCS 5/9-104.2, and change "read as an excerpt" to read whole 2026-11-08.
23. `pet-policy-il`: Illinois item 6 (backlog, Maryland flag 5(a)). Entry: the clause already limits entry "only as this Lease's Access & Entry terms permit, or without notice in an emergency". The tagged "landlords-access" gives 24 hours' notice. Illinois has no entry statute ("edu-no-entry-statute-il"), and no Illinois statute gives a landlord a right to enter or remove a tenant's animal (a screen of the Animal Control Act, 510 ILCS 5, and the Humane Care for Animals Act, 510 ILCS 70, found no landlord, tenant or lease word). So entry is lawful as written. Removal: "will not seize or remove a pet except with the help of animal control or law enforcement or through a court process" can be read to let a landlord take a tenant's animal itself, if an officer is present, without a court order. Illinois prohibits entry onto land 'except in cases where entry is allowed by law', and then only 'in a peaceable manner' (735 ILCS 5/9-101); case law on landlord self-help was not read. Proposed wording, after "pet-policy-md": "and will not seize or remove a pet except through a court process; Landlord may report a pet to animal control or law enforcement, which may act under their own authority." Pet rent: a pet first approved mid-lease creates a recurring non-optional fee that is not on the lease's first page, so under 765 ILCS 705/35(b) the tenant 'shall not be liable for payment of such fee'. Builder: when a pet is added during the term, have the parties sign an amended first page (fee box) listing the pet rent, not only a pet addendum. Both changes are for Claude Code at sync.
24. `early-termination-ks`: Illinois item 8(a). IL is tagged on this row, whose fee is "one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater". Illinois has no statute that sets or limits a residential early-termination fee (Battery IL-M-008 (whole ILCS (3,486 acts), Constitution and 104th GA Public Act list, 74290 units, corpus 2026-11-08, run 2026-11-08; controls passed: nonsense 0, "General Assembly" 5298; synthetic positive passed; positive 765 ILCS 705/16 passed): "early termination fee" 24 hits (heading-only 1); "terminate near fee" 680 hits (heading-only 6); "terminate near fee [AND]" 911 hits (heading-only 7); the bare-term hits are pension, utility, consumer-lease, motor-vehicle-lease and assisted-housing sections, plus 765 ILCS 745/6(h), a mobile home park's damages cap, and 330 ILCS 62/5-25, which bars 'any liquidated damages due to the early termination' for a servicemember's statutory termination). The landlord 'shall take reasonable measures to mitigate the damages recoverable against a defaulting lessee' (735 ILCS 5/9-213.1). Penalty doctrine is case law, not read (class decision 2). A fee that grows with the remaining Term rests only on unread case law, so the cautious course is a flat fee. Proposed row "early-termination-il" below (supersedes "early-termination-ks" for IL), following "early-termination-ga", "early-termination-nc", "early-termination-va" and "early-termination-pa". Claude Code applies it at sync.
25. `casualty-termination-il`: Illinois item 8(c). The clause's exclusion turns on casualty "not caused by Tenant, members of Tenant's household or Tenant's guests" (any causation, fault or not). "tenant-caused-damage-il" turns on "the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, a member of Tenant's household, or Tenant's guest". So a casualty the tenant side caused without fault gets neither the exit nor rent abatement, and neither clause covers it. Proposed first sentence: "If the property is damaged or destroyed by fire or other casualty that does not result from the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, a member of Tenant's household, or Tenant's guest, and the damage makes the property unfit to live in or substantially impairs Tenant's use of it, either Landlord or Tenant may end this Lease by written notice to the other, effective on the date Tenant moves out." The rest of the clause is unchanged. With this, every casualty falls under exactly one clause: fault on the tenant side goes to tenant-caused-damage-il, anything else gives the exit and abatement. No Illinois casualty statute (IL batteries 89, 97, 98).
26. `edu-notices-to-quit-il`: Illinois item 8(d), CARES. "Fixed term: no notice is needed; the tenant must leave when the term ends" and "Once a tenancy is ended by one of these notices, no further demand is needed before filing" state Illinois law correctly. For a covered property, though, the federal rule in "edu-cares-act-notice" (tagged IL) bars requiring the tenant to vacate until 30 days after a notice to vacate (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c), not read in this pass). Read alone, the row could be taken to say no notice is ever needed. Proposed added sentence: "If your property has a federally backed mortgage or is in a covered federal housing program, federal law may require 30 days' written notice to vacate first (see the federal CARES Act row)." Pointer only; no contradiction in Illinois law.
27. `edu-nonpayment-notice-il`: Illinois item 8(d), CARES. "may treat the lease as ended and file an eviction without further notice or demand" is right under 735 ILCS 5/9-209, but for a CARES-covered property a 30-day notice to vacate may be required ("edu-cares-act-notice"). The 5-day demand can be served with, or followed by, a 30-day notice to vacate. Proposed added sentence: "For a property with a federally backed mortgage or in a covered federal program, give a 30-day notice to vacate as well before filing (see the federal CARES Act row)."
28. `edu-expedited-criminal-eviction-il`: Illinois item 8(d), CARES. The 5-day notice to vacate after the lease is voided (735 ILCS 5/9-120) and the no-predicate-notice emergency procedure (735 ILCS 5/9-118, 735 ILCS 5/9-119) may be shorter than the federal 30-day notice to vacate for a covered property ("edu-cares-act-notice"; courts disagree about its reach, case law not reviewed). Proposed added sentence as for "edu-notices-to-quit-il". "criminal-activity-notice-il", "periodic-tenancy-notice-il" and "edu-eviction-process-il" don't contradict "edu-cares-act-notice": the first two give no notice period shorter than federal law on their own terms (the periodic clause gives 30 days), and the eviction-process row describes court procedure only.
29. `edu-towing-il`: The row says Vehicle Code Chapter 18a was not read. Slice H read 625 ILCS 5/18a-302 (the relocation-towing sign rule and its residential exception) for "edu-no-parking-rules-notice-il" in this delta. Proposed change: add a pointer to "edu-no-parking-rules-notice-il" and drop the not-read statement for 625 ILCS 5/18a-302; leave the rest of Chapter 18a as not read unless Claude Code reads it at sync.
30. `edu-statutory-forms-il`: Its list of statute-required lease forms and disclosures omits two that slice I found and opened: (1) where a wind or solar noise waiver is recorded for the property, the owner must disclose it to a tenant before a new lease (415 ILCS 5/25; 55 ILCS 5/5-12020); (2) a landlord or agent who is a licensed real estate broker must disclose licensee status in writing (225 ILCS 454/10-27; 225 ILCS 454/10-30(c)). Proposed change: add both to the list with pointers to "noise-waiver-disclosure-il" and "licensee-status-disclosure-il" in this delta; note that 225 ILCS 454 is scheduled for repeal on January 1, 2030 (legal watch).
31. `edu-subsidized-housing-notices-il (proposed by slice E)`: Cross-link: tenants of developments financed by the Illinois Housing Development Authority have a first right to purchase when the owner prepays the mortgage (20 ILCS 3805/8.1), answered by slice I under the tpa topics in this delta. When Claude Code adds slice E's proposed row on 765 ILCS 925, point it to slice I's tpa row and back.
32. `fee-disclosure-first-page-il`: Independent check round 1 (rule 26): the fee box prompt '[list each non-optional fee, e.g. application fee, ...]' does not say in its text that a fee barred by 765 ILCS 705/35(c) may not be listed or that an application fee is capped at $50 ('A fee for a rental application, including background checks, in excess of $50.' (765 ILCS 705/35(c)(1))); only the notes do. Proposed change: add to the bracket "never list a fee barred by the rental fee law; an application fee may not exceed $50 except a third-party background-check cost the law allows".
33. `animal-care-access-vi`: Tagged IL in this pass. The shared row's title reads "Animal Care in Tenant's Absence (Virgin Islands)"; if the product shows titles, Illinois users see another jurisdiction's name. A shared title edit, for Claude Code at sync.
34. Proposed row `edu-trust-fund-pets-il`: Illinois statutory pet rule for Affordable Housing Trust Fund units (310 ILCS 65/10(o), 310 ILCS 65/18) has no row; topic pet-policy, which Illinois already answers with pet-policy-il. Found by slice C while answering pet-eviction-fact-sheet (Battery IL-C-036).

   ```
   id: edu-trust-fund-pets-il
   group: Pets
   title: Pets in Illinois Affordable Housing Trust Fund Units
   states: IL
   is_active: TRUE
   supersedes: 
   bodyText: If a multifamily rental unit of 500 square feet or more was acquired, built or rehabilitated with money from the Illinois Affordable Housing Trust Fund after January 1, 2022 and is designated as affordable housing for low- and very low-income families, the tenant must be allowed to keep at least 2 cats or one dog weighing under 50 pounds, whatever its breed or height, in the home, in line with other State laws. The rule doesn't cover service animals (which have their own protections), a dog found dangerous or vicious under the Animal Control Act, supportive living facilities, or elderly housing (housing for people 62 or older, housing where at least 80% of units have someone 55 or older, or a qualifying government program for the elderly). Your pet policy for these units may require compliance with noise and sanitation standards, registration of the pet with you, restraint in common areas, prompt waste removal, vaccination and sterilization, and enforcement of violations. You are not liable for injuries caused by a tenant's permitted household pet except for willful and wanton misconduct. Any pet deposit is part of the security deposit, and any non-optional pet fee or pet rent must appear on the lease's first page.
   rule_type: CONSTRAINED
   content_type: LANDLORD_EDUCATION
   verification_status: VERIFIED
   notes: IL: Status: Present. 310 ILCS 65/10(o) (P.A. 102-283, eff. 1-1-22): the tenant 'shall be allowed to keep at least 2 cats or one dog that weighs under 50 pounds regardless of breed or height', with exclusions for service animals, dangerous or vicious dogs, supportive living facilities and elderly housing; 310 ILCS 65/18(a) (permitted policy terms), 310 ILCS 65/18(b) (no liability except willful and wanton misconduct), 310 ILCS 65/18(c) (accommodation laws preserved). Pet deposit as security deposit and first-page fees: "pet-policy-il", "fee-disclosure-first-page-il" (765 ILCS 705/35(b); log §4 class decision 3). Assistance animals: "edu-assistance-animals-il". Found through Battery IL-C-036. Read section-open 2026-11-08 (corpus-IL-20261108, ilga.gov ILCS compiled through P.A. 104-854): 310 ILCS 65/10, 310 ILCS 65/18, 765 ILCS 705/35.
   effective_from: 2026-11-08
   last_checked: 2026-11-08
   choice_group: 
   is_default: 
   topic_key: pet-policy
   lease_clause_basis:
   ```

35. Proposed row `edu-subsidized-housing-notices-il`: A statutory pre-lease and tenant notice duty with a $1,000 tenant penalty (765 ILCS 925, the Notice of Prepayment of Federally Subsidized Mortgage Act) has no IL row. It sits outside slice E's topics, so it is filed as a proposal. Topic key suggested: conversion-notice, following Washington's "edu-subsidized-housing-notice-wa" (a federal-contract ending notice keyed there); Illinois already has "edu-condo-conversion-il" under that key as an education row, so the main pass may prefer required-disclosures or a new key for subsidized-housing notices (rule 57).

   ```
   id: edu-subsidized-housing-notices-il
   group: Disclosures
   title: Notices in Federally Subsidized Buildings
   states: IL
   is_active: TRUE
   supersedes: 
   bodyText: If you own federally subsidized housing (a building with a HUD or Farmers Home mortgage, mortgage insurance or rent subsidy under the listed federal programs, or a Section 8 contract), Illinois requires written notices to tenants. Before signing any lease in a building with a Section 8 contract that the Illinois Housing Development Authority does not administer, give each prospective tenant written notice of the date the Section 8 contract expires. If you may prepay the federally insured or held mortgage, post a notice in the building and deliver it personally or by certified mail to every tenant at least 12 months before the earliest prepayment date, and give a copy to every prospective tenant; if you intend to prepay, give a second notice at least 9 months before, posted and delivered the same way and given in writing to prospective tenants during the notice period. Both notices go to the local clerk and the Illinois Housing Development Authority too, on forms the Authority prescribes. An owner who fails to give a required tenant notice owes that tenant $1,000. Federal program rules may require more and are not covered here.
   rule_type: RECOMMENDED
   content_type: LANDLORD_EDUCATION
   verification_status: VERIFIED
   notes: IL: Status: Present. Controlling text read: 'Before executing any lease agreement, an owner of a building receiving a Section 8 contract, of which IHDA is not the contract administrator, shall notify all prospective tenants in writing of the date of the expiration of the Section 8 contract' (765 ILCS 925/6); the 12-month notice must be posted and delivered 'personally or by certified mail' to all tenants, and 'The owner shall provide a copy of the notice to all prospective tenants' (765 ILCS 925/4(b)); the 9-month intent notice 'shall be provided in writing to all prospective tenants during the notice period' (765 ILCS 925/5); 'An owner who fails to provide the notice to a tenant as required under Sections 5 and 6 of this Act shall pay a penalty to the tenant in the amount of $1,000' (765 ILCS 925/7); definitions of owner, Section 8 contract and subsidized housing in 765 ILCS 925/3. Found as a heading-only hit of Battery IL-E-019 and read whole. No library row cites 765 ILCS 925 (CSV search for the act number: 0). Federal rules (HUD notice of contract expiration and opt-out) not read (log §4 class decision 11). Read section-open 2026-11-08 (corpus-IL-20261108, ilga.gov ILCS compiled through P.A. 104-854): 765 ILCS 925 (whole Act: 765 ILCS 925/1 to 765 ILCS 925/8).
   effective_from: 2026-11-08
   last_checked: 2026-11-08
   choice_group: 
   is_default: 
   topic_key: conversion-notice
   lease_clause_basis:
   ```

36. Proposed row `early-termination-il`: Illinois item 8(a): override of early-termination-ks for IL (flat one-month fee). IL is to be removed from early-termination-ks's states at sync.

   ```
   id: early-termination-il
   group: Default & Termination
   title: Early Termination
   states: IL
   is_active: TRUE
   supersedes: early-termination-ks
   bodyText: Tenant may terminate this Lease before the end of the Term by providing Landlord at least 30 days' written notice. Tenant will pay an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) and remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to the termination date. If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy ends on the notice that applicable law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. Landlord may terminate this Lease early in accordance with this Lease's Tenant Default and notice provisions, or if Tenant vacates or abandons the property without notifying Landlord. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, including a Tenant's right to terminate without penalty due to military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act or Illinois law, under the Safe Homes Act, under the Landlord and Tenant Act's flood-disclosure rules, due to the property becoming uninhabitable through no fault of Tenant, or any other termination right or limitation provided by applicable law.
   rule_type: REQUIRED
   content_type: LEASE_CLAUSE
   verification_status: VERIFIED
   notes: IL: Override of "early-termination-ks" (IL gaps pass 2026-11-08, Illinois item 8(a)). Present. Optional exit for a fixed Term at a flat fee of one month's Rent. No Illinois statute sets or limits a residential early-termination fee (Battery IL-M-008 (whole ILCS (3,486 acts), Constitution and 104th GA Public Act list, 74290 units, corpus 2026-11-08, run 2026-11-08; controls passed: nonsense 0, "General Assembly" 5298; synthetic positive passed; positive 765 ILCS 705/16 passed): "early termination fee" 24 hits (heading-only 1); "terminate near fee" 680 hits (heading-only 6); "terminate near fee [AND]" 911 hits (heading-only 7)). Penalty doctrine is case law, not read, so the library uses one month's Rent rather than a percentage of the remaining Rent that grows with the Term (log §4 class decision 2). Mitigation: 'a landlord or his or her agent shall take reasonable measures to mitigate the damages recoverable against a defaulting lessee' (735 ILCS 5/9-213.1). Statutory exits kept by the savings sentence: 765 ILCS 705/16 (military orders); 330 ILCS 62/5-15 to 330 ILCS 62/5-25 (Service Member Residential Property Act; 'not liable for any other rent or any liquidated damages due to the early termination'); 765 ILCS 750/15 (Safe Homes Act defense to rent after a tenant vacates); 765 ILCS 705/25(e) (termination where the flood disclosure was not given); FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955 (not read in this pass). The fee is the tenant's option under the lease, not a 'fee or fine for modification or renewal of a lease agreement' (765 ILCS 705/35(c)(3)). It is a charge that applies only if the tenant elects it; the builder lists it in the first-page fee box with the conditional charges (log §4 class decision 3). The Rent-after-termination sentence follows "early-termination-ga". No new variables. Read section-open 2026-11-08 (corpus-IL-20261108, ilga.gov ILCS compiled through P.A. 104-854): 735 ILCS 5/9-213.1; 765 ILCS 705/16; 765 ILCS 705/25; 765 ILCS 705/35; 765 ILCS 750/15; 330 ILCS 62/5-25.
   effective_from: 2026-11-08
   last_checked: 2026-11-08
   choice_group: 
   is_default: 
   topic_key: early-termination
   lease_clause_basis: SERVES_LANDLORD
   ```

None.

#### New builder variables

None.

### Proposed SOP changes

1. **Battery heading-only rule (rule 19; found by slice J).** The battery tool treats a section's first sentence as its catchline when the section has none, so a match only in that sentence was reported as heading-only and not counted in hits. Proposed: count a match as heading-only only where the section prints a real catchline, and have the tool flag any heading-only hit whose "heading" is longer than 90 characters or contains a verb. In this pass the main pass screened every logged battery (211 such sections) and confirmed every low-count term's citing row deals with the section (log §4, decision on slice J). Logged counts were left as run so the replay reproduces.
2. **Clear the fetch queue between batches.** Helpers filed `fetch` objects for sources outside the corpus; the main pass read them in the browser after each batch and revised the rows by override, so only fetches no row relied on were left at delivery. Proposed: make that the standard flow, and require that a delivered row is never NEEDS_REVIEW for a fetch the main pass could have read in the browser.
3. **Administrative Code channel.** The JCAR Administrative Code database on ilga.gov (`/agencies/JCAR/EntirePart?titlepart=TTTPPPPP`) reads whole parts; old JCAR URLs return 404. The site says its text is not the official text. Proposed: record that disclaimer in §1 whenever a row quotes it, and hash-check every copy (SHA-256 of the whitespace-collapsed text in the browser and the container), as this pass did.
4. **uscode.house.gov's temporary format.** The old `view.xhtml?req=granuleid:...` links return 404; sections are at `/us/usc/t<title>/s<section>/`. Proposed: update the federal-sources note.
5. **Quote screen and apostrophes.** The validator's single-quote span check splits on an apostrophe inside a quote ("homeowners' association"), which forced paraphrase. Proposed: allow a quote delimited by `‘…’` or a marked span so a source apostrophe never breaks verification.
6. **Pointer screen.** A whole-delta screen for row ids named in notes that exist neither in the delta nor in the library found two dangling pointers. Proposed: add that screen to the validator, with an allowance for ids proposed in §6.

### Proposed topic questions

Questions this pass found worth asking of every state, by topic key:
- `pool-safety`: Does a health statute license pools at apartment or condominium buildings above a unit count, even though the act otherwise reaches only "public" pools? (Illinois: 210 ILCS 125/3.01, 5 or more living units.)
- `lead-based-paint`: After a state lead order on a unit is complied with and certified, must the owner still give each new tenant written notice that a hazard was once found there? (Illinois: 77 Ill. Adm. Code 845.25(a).) Must occupants get notice a set number of days before abatement work starts? (Illinois: 77 Ill. Adm. Code 845.250(b), 7 to 30 days.)
- `habitability`: Is it a crime for an owner or manager to recklessly let a rental become dangerous, with a prosecutor's rent escrow for repairs? (Illinois: 720 ILCS 5/12-5.1, 5/12-5.2.)
- `landlord-breach-remedy`: Does a lead or health statute direct rent withholding until the owner complies, with an eviction bar? (Illinois: 410 ILCS 45/10.)
- `utility-disconnection-notice-authorization`: Do the utility commission's rules let a residential customer name a third party (such as the landlord) to receive duplicate disconnection notices? (Illinois: 83 Ill. Adm. Code 280.130(f).)
- `optional-lease-terms` / `required-disclosures`: Does a lease with an option to purchase make the landlord a seller who owes the state's property disclosure report, and when? (Illinois: 765 ILCS 77/10.)
- `required-disclosures`: Must a recorded wind or solar noise waiver be disclosed to a tenant before a new lease? (Illinois: 415 ILCS 5/25; 55 ILCS 5/5-12020.) Must a landlord or agent who is a licensed broker disclose that status in writing? (Illinois: 225 ILCS 454/10-27, 10-30(c).)
- `private-well`: Is a well serving rental housing a regulated "semi-private" system with a posting duty when water fails a standard? (Illinois: 415 ILCS 55/9(a)(7), (m).)
- `tenant-bankruptcy`: State the federal automatic-stay exceptions (11 U.S.C. § 362(b)(22)-(23), (l), (m)) and lease rejection timing (11 U.S.C. § 365(d)) once, as a shared federal education row, so each state row can point to it.

**Decisions.** The log asked Taylor no questions. Arbitration is not offered: 765 ILCS 705/35(f) penalizes a lease that requires a barred fee (Taylor's 2026-11-07 rule). Decision B1 (tag fee-bearing clauses whose only Illinois condition is first-page listing, relying on the fee box every Illinois lease prints) is kept, by precedent: rule 26 asks for the condition in lease text, and `fee-disclosure-first-page-il` states it there ("Tenant is not required to pay any non-optional fee that is not listed on this page"); Utah's and Alabama's overrides answered statutes with no such box. P.A. 104-479's effective date stays unresolved (July 1, 2026 under its Section 99, January 1, 2027 in the General Assembly's records); the rows take the cautious course.

## Rule type: early termination (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12: `early-termination-il` is now `rule_type` RECOMMENDED, not REQUIRED, with the shared `early-termination-ks` and every state version that copied its label (OH, NV, TX, NJ, AZ, GA, SC, NC, VA, PA, IL). Under SOP rule 56, REQUIRED means a statute requires the clause in the lease, and no state's does. The label is research metadata only; no clause text changed. Taylor's lesson 2 (flag a lease without an early-termination clause) stays as a builder warning in the backlog.

## Federal row: HUD assistance-animal enforcement change (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12: the assistance-animal clause stays as written (an animal a tenant needs for a disability, trained or not, is an accommodation, not a pet), and a new federal education row, `edu-assistance-animals-federal-enforcement`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction but CO. It explains HUD's September 17, 2025 withdrawal of its assistance-animal guidance (91 Fed. Reg. 17291) and FHEO's May 22, 2026 memorandum limiting Fair Housing Act charges to trained animals, and what neither changes: the Act, 24 C.F.R. § 100.204, private suits, Section 504 and ADA complaints, and state law. No clause text changed.
